L176Q - Mon 7 Apr 1997 / Lun 7 avr
CITY OF TORONTO ACT, 1996 / LOI DE 1996 SUR LA CITÉ DE TORONTO
Report continued from volume P.
1602
CITY OF TORONTO ACT, 1996 / LOI DE 1996 SUR LA CITÉ DE TORONTO
Continuing consideration of Bill 103, An Act to replace the seven existing municipal governments of Metropolitan Toronto by incorporating a new municipality to be known as the City of Toronto / Projet de loi 103, Loi visant à remplacer les sept administrations municipales existantes de la communauté urbaine de Toronto en constituant une nouvelle municipalité appelée la cité de Toronto.
The First Deputy Chair (Ms Marilyn Churley):
NDP motion, subsection 24(4): Oakhurst Drive. Shall the motion carry? All those in favour please say "aye." Those opposed please say "nay." In my opinion the nays have it. The vote is lost.

Mr Mario Sergio (Yorkview):
On a point of order, Madam Chair: I didn't catch you in the middle of a roll. A previous one, the second to this one here, was withdrawn by the member of the NDP as being a misspelled or incorrect name. Omagh is in my riding and I know it's a correct name and the correct spelling. I know it's a correct name, it's the correct spelling, and I wouldn't want the people living on Omagh to think that they're living on a wrong or misspelled street. I would like to have that for the record because it does exist. It's the proper spelling.
1620
The First Deputy Chair:
Thank you. Can we have unanimous consent? No? Okay. It's on the record. Thank you very much for pointing that out. I urge all members to point out any inconsistencies in that way. It's important to have them on the record.
Mr Wayne Wettlaufer (Kitchener):
Point of order, Madam Chair: I was just wondering who raised that last point of order. My understanding of the rules is that everyone is supposed to sit down when there's a point of order. I wonder whether the member for Lawrence was reading that point of order or whether he was throwing his voice.
The First Deputy Chair:
Member for Kitchener, the only time, as far as I understand, where the rules are clear about members standing is when the Speaker is standing. We all know that at that time everybody else should be sitting. It is my discretion as the Chair when I'm taking a point of order, to determine whether the person is actually making a point of order, notwithstanding that somebody else may be standing. It was obvious to me that the member for Yorkview was making the point of order. Thank you, however, for your point of order.
Mr Marchese:
Point of privilege, Madam Chair: I have to say I felt sincerely that I had made an error. Omaha and Omagh may have appeared to be the same to me, so I want to apologize to the residents of Omagh, because they do indeed exist, according to what the member says. I apologize to the residents of Omagh for that mistake.
The First Deputy Chair:
Thank you very much. It's not a point of order but I think it's good to have to have it on the record.

Legislative Assembly of Ontario
The Legislative Assembly of Ontario is made up of members of provincial Parliament (MPPs) who are elected by Ontarians to represent them and their constituencies. The Legislative Assembly's main responsibilities are to debate and pass legislation, to hold the government to account, and to approve government spending. The Legislative Assembly meets in the Legislative Building located in Queen's Park in Toronto.
